# Restore preparation and audit

Status: shared-state live test passed. A separate one-shot native full-serializer
round-trip is now implemented, tested with mock routines, and built locally.
It is installed; the first native full-serializer idle test completed. Full restoration has
run once at the same update boundary in SC6; neither exact state equivalence, snapshot completeness, nor rewind correctness is proven.

## Verified findings

For executable SHA-256
F8904E4B04BCA3B47BC52A683F6190365D2EB89EE8F44F8072759E9C5E04A553:

- Top-level reader RVA 384540 calls the two player readers, relinks players with
  309ED0, and loads shared state and manager state in the writer's broad order.
- 321A30 reads signed type IDs and dispatches IDs 0 through 8 to reconstruction
  routines before virtual reader slot 108. Missing type -1 is skipped. These
  routines use manager-owned slots, clear their memory through 311C070, install
  type vtables, call initialization slots C8/D0/D8, and update manager indexing.
  This is object reconstruction, not just copying bytes back.
- 3249E0 preserves the current auxiliary object's vtable and buffer pointer
  around its 2F0-byte read, reads 41E0 bytes into that buffer, then uses restored
  child pointers for 17 virtual-reader dispatches. Old captures could contain
  stale child pointers. Same-process alone is insufficient to validate lifetime.
- Player reader 30AE80 preserves fields 244/248/324; calls animation rebuild
  routines 349290 and 2F8FA0; reconstructs internal pointers; and calls the
  interface at global 470D188 through slot C0.
- That interface currently resolves to 400760. It converts an event via 3EB3A0
  and synchronously dispatches through 1D38300 at interface+A18. This is not
  merely a queue write. Four listeners were observed in read-only runtime checks.
- Listener wrapper 3EF060 resolves a weak object and invokes its bound method.
  Observed bound methods: 3C6050 (two listeners), 5112A0, 3C6020. 3C6050 updates
  object+450 and invokes 3C90B0 when the event targets it. 5112A0 jumps to
  4FB230, which touches reference-counted Unreal-side objects and calls other
  routines. 3C6020 returns for the zero event flag supplied by the inspected
  player-load paths. Full callback side-effect coverage remains unverified.

PE unwind entries split several routines into fragments. Analysis must follow
branches across those entries; the first unwind range is not a whole function.
Data following jump tables is not executable code even if linear disassembly
prints instructions for it.

## Implemented local preparation

src/RestoreBuffer.hpp is a local-only bounded reader, not a game ABI adapter.
It uses the saved byte length, rejects reads before copying if the range exceeds
that length, retains the first failure, and requires exact stream consumption.
It throws in local tests; unwinding this exception through game code is explicitly
not supported. The game ignores read return values, so returning zero alone would
allow it to use uninitialized relocation/type data and continue changing state.
A live failure strategy must be resolved before connecting this reader to SC6.

scripts/Test-RestoreBuffer.ps1 builds tests/RestoreBufferTest.cpp with MSVC
/W4 /WX. Passed: exact consumption, destination guards, truncated input, maximal
integer request, sticky rejection, null destination, copy failure, and partial
consumption. This does not validate the game's loader or undo partial writes.

## First live experiment design

1. Offline Training, idle, supported hash, verified boundary and expected runtime
   methods. Reject concurrent or repeated restore requests.
2. Create a fresh in-memory save and validate every transfer. Do not load a .bin
   from disk or a different session.
3. Save and load at the same boundary before advancing simulation; retain object
   identities and node counts. This tests the loader before attempting a rewind.
4. Record read byte/transfer counts, elapsed time, selected fields before/after,
   and a subsequent save. Raw byte equality alone is insufficient because padding
   and intentionally preserved/rebuilt fields can differ.
5. Verify subsequent frame continuity and user-visible behavior. Stop on any
   mismatch; no automatic second restore or assumption that exceptions undo writes.

Before enabling step 3, finish tracing reconstruction initialization/read methods
and external listener effects, define a failure strategy for partially applied
loads, and validate the actual game ABI adapter in a disposable test process.
Delayed restoration and deterministic replay remain later experiments.

Evidence: work/restore-audit.txt, restore-manager.txt,
restore-reconstruction.txt, restore-event-callback.txt, restore-event-queue.txt,
restore-listener-dispatch.txt, restore-bound-listeners.txt,
restore-listener-bodies.txt; captures/restore-objects-54696-01.json and
restore-listeners-54696-01.json / -02.json. Runtime inspection used read-only
process access and invoked no game routines.

## Narrow shared-state experiment (2026-10-07)

The full loader remains blocked on reconstruction and external callback effects.
The separate SharedRestoreProbe calls only writer 31DC00 and reader 31DE50.
Static audit in work/restore-shared.txt confirms five raw blocks and four pointer
relocations; no player reconstruction or event listener dispatch is on this path.
The raw blocks are 48462D0/58, 4846330/140, 48545A8/50, 48545F8/EF0, 48545A0/8
(addresses and sizes hexadecimal). Four 16-byte relocation records describe
4846470..4846488. Total stream length is 4384 bytes in nine transfers.

A fresh save is loaded immediately at the same before-2DBC60 boundary. All four
relocation indices, bounds, and decoded original pointers are checked before
calling the reader. Expected transfer lengths and actual stream bounds are
checked at each read. A structured exception stops an unexpected read instead
of letting game code continue with invalid data. This does not undo partial
writes on failure. The recorder stops on a failed probe and further shared
restore requests are rejected for the rest of that process.

The comparison checks all five raw blocks, all four decoded pointers, and the
outer counter. It excludes uninitialized relocation padding. Subsequent 600-row
recording checks continuity. This is neither a delayed rewind nor a complete
snapshot, determinism test, or full rollback implementation.

Trigger (only when offline Training is confirmed, unpaused and idle):

```powershell
& .\scripts\Arm-Recorder.ps1 -TrainingConfirmed -SharedRestore
```

The PID-bound offline-shared-restore.arm token is distinct from save and record
requests. At most one shared request is accepted per process. Reports use
shared-restore-PID-TICK-restore.txt alongside the CSV and recorder summary.
No snapshot is loaded from disk.

Test-SharedRestore.ps1 passed under MSVC /W4 /WX: a mock round-trip restores
intentionally changed destinations, invalid relocation indices prevent load,
unexpected writes and oversized reads raise trapped failures, state mismatch
is reported, invalid base is caught, and a second invocation is inert. Native
build also passed existing recorder/bridge and save-stream tests. The subsequent
live shared-state validation is recorded below.

Installed build SHA-256: 1031933EE74E193C25B54EBAA3A60D5A45BF3C3ACA0A51063141E4538EEC41D6.
Save-only DLL preserved as dlls/main-before-shared-restore.dll.disabled. No pending arm files at installation.


## First live shared-state result

Capture shared-restore-56848-19444812: user confirmed offline Training, unpaused and idle. Native writer and reader each completed nine transfers, 4384 bytes. All compared raw blocks and decoded pointers matched; outer counter remained 393 across the immediate round-trip. No fault, exception, or transfer failure. Elapsed 30 ticks at 10 MHz (3 microseconds). Subsequent CSV independently checked: 600 rows, zero outer-counter discontinuities; recorder reported zero skipped entries. Windows reported the process responding. User confirmed the game remained responsive, movement was normal, and no visible glitches occurred. This does not test delayed rewind or full match restoration.



## Follow-up full-loader preflight (PID 56848)

Ran read-only serializer and callback inventories after the successful shared
round-trip. Reports: captures/restore-objects-56848-01.json and
captures/restore-listeners-56848-01.json. Serializer inventory returned no errors;
player pointers were stable across inspection and matched the recorder. The four
registered bound methods still match the previous session (3C6050 twice,
5112A0, 3C6020). This establishes method identity, not loader correctness.

Expanded static inspection now covers 3C90B0 through return 3C9C46 and 4FB230
through return 4FB591, rather than stopping at their short initial unwind ranges.
Evidence: work/restore-listener-expanded.txt and
captures/restore-callback-audit-56848-01.json. The former also includes adjoining
routines for context; the JSON call-site inventory is bounded to these returns.

3C90B0 contains 60 call sites, 24 indirect. It clears/rebuilds an object-owned
collection at +470, traverses components at +3B0, calls component slot +308 with
both zero and one, replaces the allocation at +510, and calls attachment-like
routine 1D92B60 followed by component slot +3A8. The precise semantic names of
these routines are not established; their mutations extend beyond raw battle
snapshot blocks. Counting call sites does not mean every branch runs on a load.

4FB230 contains 15 call sites, six indirect. It copies reference-counted pairs
from object +400, queries player-related objects, and calls 55B4B0 with the UTF-16
parameter name Special_Parts_Type and a computed float. This narrows the observed
callback to a parameter-update path, but does not establish that all downstream
effects are idempotent or captured by the battle serializer.

No additional restore was armed or performed during this preflight. The installed
DLL still exposes only the already-tested shared restore scope. Next full-loader
work must resolve the live component dispatches, measure or account for their
external state, and implement/test the full stream adapter before a full match
restore can be run. Do not bypass the one-shared-restore-per-process guard to
present a repeated shared test as full-state progress.

## Component dispatch resolution and full probe implementation

Read-only capture restore-components-56848-01.json resolved both player callback
weak targets using the inspected F823F0 object-array/serial/flag checks. Player
sides 0/1 contained 22/21 component entries. Their slot 308 resolved to 1D5DBE0
and slot 3A8 to 1DACA60. Both attachment arrays at +520 had count zero.
Player target slots 5F8/600/680/698 resolved to 3BF540/3BF8A0/3BE3A0/3BE670.
The inventory is explicitly non-atomic and did not invoke these methods.

Static evidence work/restore-component-methods.txt shows slot 308 conditionally
updates a tick-related structure at +110 through 2167500; slot 3A8 compares and
updates a three-float vector at +300 and can propagate transform work. Direct
routine 1DAD440 changes bit 0x10 at component +240, calls virtual slot 418, and
walks child components. These are external component effects, not raw snapshot
copies. Names like tick/transform are interpretation; offsets and call targets
are directly observed. Not all descendant effects have been proven idempotent.

The revised experiment is a one-shot native full-serializer compatibility probe,
not proof of full state coverage. It exercises the original callbacks rather
than skipping them. Same-boundary operation avoids deliberately stale pointers,
but reconstruction/callbacks can still crash or leave visible state changes.
It must remain a disposable offline Training test; a failure requires restarting
the game, not retrying or assuming partial writes were undone.

FullRestoreStream.hpp preserves the Win64 stream prefix and records up to 4096
write lengths. Only a successful fresh save can be sealed for reading. Every
read must match the next transfer size and remain within actual written bytes.
Unexpected reset/seek/delete/write-after-seal, copy faults, and overreads raise a
structured exception to stop the current native invocation. Bounds do not prove
semantic correctness of a native loader or make arbitrary game exceptions safe.

FullStateProbe.hpp verifies save-side and reader-side method pairs, manager
initializers, manager slot identity, linked-node readers, auxiliary readers,
four callback bindings, weak-target validity, player component methods, and idle
input/move/speed conditions before writing a snapshot. It runs native writer
3841E0, seals the stream, rechecks preflight, invokes reader 384540 once, checks
exact consumption, selected globals and player pointers, then takes a second save
if postflight checks pass. Known writer bookkeeping is preserved. The recorder
also compares all its selected global/player/animation fields across the probe.
These selected comparisons do not cover all engine component state.

Raw before/after binaries are diagnostics: padding, reconstructed pointers, and
intentional native changes can differ. Raw differences are reported, not treated
as a determinism verdict. The first live run must be interpreted before any
rewind test. Phase codes: 1 preflight, 2 save, 3 load, 4 postflight, 5 second save,
6 local probe steps completed. Any probe failure stops recording and blocks all
new captures for that process; successful full probes also cannot run twice.

```powershell
& .\scripts\Arm-Recorder.ps1 -TrainingConfirmed -FullRestore
```

Requires installed/staged hashes to match and a unique PID-bound
OFFLINE_FULL_RESTORE arm token. Never imports a saved file. Reports are
full-restore-PID-TICK-restore.txt, -before.bin, -after.bin (only when valid), plus
the normal CSV/summary. Stay unpaused and idle in offline Training throughout.

Test-FullRestore.ps1 passed with MSVC /W4 /WX: changed mock destination restored,
preflight/idle rejection prevents loading, oversized and mismatched reads,
forbidden operations, null destination, incomplete consumption, state mismatch,
postflight rejection, preserved bookkeeping, once-only invocation, and invalid
base. Native build passed recorder/bridge, save stream, shared restore, and full
probe tests. These local mocks do not execute SC6's native loader.

## First native full-serializer live result

Capture full-restore-66012-20201703 (offline Training, idle at the measured restore boundary; attacks during subsequent recording): preflight passed;
writer and reader each completed 1310 transfers and 106766 bytes. Postflight and
second save passed. No reported fault, exception, or transfer failures. Selected
keys, player pointers, and all recorder fields matched immediately after load.
The outer counter remained 594 across the probe. Duration: 9814 ticks at 10 MHz,
0.9814 ms. The following 600 rows had no skipped entries or outer discontinuities;
the copied CSV was independently checked. User confirmed responsiveness, normal
movement, and no visible glitches.

Before/after binary sizes match, but 222 bytes differ. They remain unclassified;
padding and native reconstruction are possible explanations, not established
causes. Captures include both binaries and a -comparison.json containing SHA-256
hashes and difference runs. This establishes successful native-loader execution
in this idle test, not exact restoration or deterministic rewind. Next work is
mapping the differences to serialized fields before delayed restore experiments.

### User correction: attacks occurred during the capture

User reported performing attacks and could not recall timing. The entire
600-frame recording must not be labeled idle. CSV inspection shows sample 0
had both inputs zero, both moves 65535, multiplier 1, and gate 0; the native
idle preflight also passed immediately before the one-shot save/load. Earliest
recorded P1 input is sample 20, 0.3332783 seconds after sample 0; earliest P1 move
is sample 31, 0.5166029 seconds after sample 0. P2 move/input first appear at
samples 56/57. The restore took 0.0009814 seconds on the first entry, so these
recorded attacks occurred afterward. This supports an idle-boundary restore
followed by activity, not restoration during combat. Exact physical input timing
is unknown. The 222 immediate before/after differences still require analysis;
later attacks cannot explain differences in the second save taken within the
same first-entry probe.

## Difference mapping completed for both live full-probe captures

Ran tools/analyze_restore_differences.py on the 161-byte clean-idle difference
and the earlier 222-byte difference. Per-field reports are saved as
captures/full-restore-38556-20426046-field-differences.json and
captures/full-restore-66012-20201703-field-differences.json. Every changed byte
mapped into a direct player block written by 30A980; none remains unmapped.
The script validates player stream origins (0 and 36860) using the rebuilt
self-pointer and captured index/state/timer/move/animation-progress fields.
It does not assume the length of variable linked-list data to find player 2.

Clean idle run, counts are differing bytes, not total field widths:

| Category | Bytes | Evidence |
| --- | ---: | --- |
| Player self-pointer at +43DF0 | 12 | Reader instruction 30B12A writes the player pointer; captured value was zero beforehand. |
| 30 animation floats per player at +97100..+97177 | 120 | 349290 calls 3493F0, whose 3494F1..349533 writes float 1.0 across two groups. Both players changed from zero to 1.0 in this run. |
| Float fields +97208 and +97218 | 14 | 2F8FA0 calls 2FA080 with mode 1; it calls RNG routine 34F550 and writes the computed float to subobject +20/+30. |
| Integer fields +9721C and +97228 | 10 | 2F909F resets +34 to -1; 2F908C zeros +40. |
| Float +97238 | 4 | 2F90A6 sets subobject +50 to 1.0. |
| P2 mode parameter +9720C | 1 | 2FA0A5 writes a mode-table value to subobject +24; observed -0.2 to +0.2. |

The earlier run has the same changed field categories: 12/180/15/10/4/1 bytes,
respectively, totaling 222. The different count is mostly different initial float
bit patterns, not attacks between the immediate before/after saves.

Critical finding: the inspected path 30B0A0 -> 2F8FA0 -> 2FA080(mode 1) -> 34F550
advances a PRNG global at RVA 485EB28 and computes new field values during load.
34F550 reads that global at 34F550 and writes it at 34F5B9. Static evidence is in
work/restore-diff-writers.txt and work/restore-rng-path.txt. Neither live probe
recorded this RNG global before/after, so its exact live transition and full
snapshot coverage must be measured rather than inferred from binary equality.

These differences are real serialized fields, not identified padding. Some are
explicit native rebuild/reset operations, but gameplay harmlessness is not
established. The previous successful execution/selected-field tests must not be
presented as exact restoration. Do not mask these ranges out of a deterministic
comparison just to make it pass. Next experiment should capture RNG state and the
affected fields, determine which are required for replay, and test a narrowly
scoped preservation strategy before any delayed rewind. No game memory was read
or changed during this disk-based difference analysis.

## RNG and affected-field measurements added

FullStateProbe now reads RNG global 485EB28 and the affected player blocks at
four checkpoints: before save, after save, after load, and after the second save.
The reads do not preserve or modify these fields; native save/load behavior is
unchanged. Each checkpoint reports validity, RNG u32, and both player pointers.
A valid checkpoint writes a 448-byte -fields-NAME.bin: 224 bytes per player,
concatenating player+43DF0 (8), +97100 (120), +971E8 (96). This separates save-side
changes from load-side changes without guessing from the two full binaries.
The four checkpoint files are companion diagnostics, not loadable snapshots.

The local mock now advances RNG only in its load routine. Tests verify identical
before/after-save RNG, the changed after-load/post-save values, no measurement
before a rejected preflight, and no telemetry-induced restoration of the RNG.
Existing recorder/bridge, save, shared restore, and full probe tests passed.

## Guarded preservation experiment implemented

Staged build 0D4525083DC07A2B5C1BBB188506383C7B3064E2DD70F9113F240291D98CB05B;
not installed yet. Enable explicitly with -TrainingConfirmed -FullRestore
-PreserveScalars in Arm-Recorder.ps1. This writes OFFLINE_FULL_PRESERVE in the
same PID-bound full arm file; output prefix is full-preserve-. Ordinary full
restore remains measurement-only. Both modes share the once-per-process guard.

After a complete native load and passing selected-state/postflight checks, the
preservation step validates: save caused no tracked changes; the seed is valid;
load produced exactly the previously observed four PRNG steps; both player
identities are unchanged; native self-pointers equal the player addresses; and
no tracked bytes changed outside the measured scalar ranges or self-pointer.
Unexpected changes reject preservation before any correction writes.

The only correction writes are RNG global 485EB28 (4 bytes), player+97100 (120
bytes), and six four-byte fields relative to player+971E8: +20, +24, +30, +34,
+40, +50. Values come from the fresh before-save checkpoint in this same entry.
It does not overwrite the rebuilt self-pointer, buffers, allocator state,
listener state, or other pointer-bearing fields. This is still an experiment:
restoring scalar values does not prove all dependent engine state is consistent.

Measurement schema 2 adds after_preservation (index 4; logical order is indices
0,1,2,4,3). It verifies all tracked non-pointer bytes match the original and the
rebuilt pointer is retained, then checks that the post-save leaves these fields,
player identities, and RNG unchanged. Reports distinguish preservation_requested,
preservation_applied (writes started), and preservation_verified. Any rejection,
fault, or unexpected post-save mutation invalidates the probe and blocks further
captures until restart. A write fault cannot automatically undo partial writes.

Local tests passed: successful scalar/RNG preservation, rebuilt-pointer retained,
unexpected field or RNG transition rejected before writes, and unexpected
post-save RNG mutation detected. Existing stream/bounds, bridge, recorder, shared,
and full probe tests also passed under the native build. Script syntax verified.
Live preservation has not run; the previous RNG-measurement DLL remains installed
until SC6 is closed for installation.

Preservation DLL installed with SC6 closed; SHA-256 verified as 0D4525083DC07A2B5C1BBB188506383C7B3064E2DD70F9113F240291D98CB05B. Capture throw-preserve-52016-24101609 matched moves 487/10041, multiplier 0,

transfers, no transfer failures or exception. Preflight/postflight and selected

stopped after one entry with full_restore_failed; no post-save was performed.
The raw_byte_differences=0 default is not an actual binary comparison here.

Offline checkpoint analysis shows save did not change tracked fields or RNG.
Load advanced RNG by the expected four steps (529928346 -> 1724161533), and
rebuilt both expected player pointers. However, two bytes outside the permitted
correction ranges changed for player 2: player+97204 byte 0 -> 1 and player+97220
byte 1 -> 0. These explain the guard rejection. Their semantics and native
writers require investigation before considering additional correction writes.
Evidence copied to captures/throw-preserve-52016-24101609*, including
-guard-analysis.json. This is a failed preservation test, not a successful
combat restore. User-visible behavior has not yet been confirmed. Restart SC6
before any further probe because native load already ran and correction did not.
## A+G rejection: native writers identified

Verified executable disassembly (work/throw-field-writers.txt, decoded from full
function starts) identifies both changes after the serialized 96-byte block is
read at RVA 30B006:

- player+97204 = subobject+1C. RVA 2F907C sets mode argument 1;
  2F9090 calls 2FA080; 2FA08C stores that argument as a 32-bit value.
- player+97220 = subobject+38. RVA 30B095..30B09D writes a 32-bit
  boolean indicating whether the pointer at subobject+0 is null.

Captured P2 values are full dwords 0 -> 1 and 1 -> 0, respectively.
P1 remains 1 and 0. Both subobject pointers are non-null and unchanged across
load. Thus the P2 +38 saved flag can differ from the loader's derived default
in a connected throw; its gameplay meaning is not yet established. These are
explicit native reinitializations after deserialization, not stream corruption.

Identifying writers does not prove that restoring these two scalar values alone
preserves combat: mode initialization also changes the referenced object's state.
No correction ranges were broadened and no second live probe was armed. Next
implementation should preserve these measured scalar values with exact loader
transition checks, retain all existing pointer/RNG/unknown-field guards, and
compare a full post-save before advancing to other moves. Further component
state coverage remains unresolved even if the binary comparison passes.

## Residual write identified exactly

Capture throw-preserve-62740-24899718 auxiliary header contains 17 object
pointers at 0x3E0 spacing. Index 4 is buffer+0xF80 (0x1448B3B5240), so the
residual buffer+0x1350 is object+0x3D0. Its captured vtable RVA 3E88858 has
reader slot +28 = RVA 32ACE0, independently verified from the pinned PE.
Associated player at object+3B8 is P1 0x7FF6D78B56F0 both before and after.
The complete 32-bit flag changes 0 -> 1, not merely an isolated padding byte.

Reader 32ACE0 restores the associated player pointer, reads the saved player
counter, adjusts object+3C8, clears object+3C0, and when the associated player
is non-null explicitly writes dword 1 to object+3D0 at RVA 32AD87.
This precisely explains the remaining serialized difference. Static evidence:
work/aux-residual-virtual-reader.txt. Captured attribution:
captures/throw-preserve-62740-24899718-residual-writer.json.

No new live probe or state write performed for this investigation. The flag's
consumers and purpose still need analysis before choosing to preserve its saved
value versus retaining loader reinitialization. Explained does not mean harmless,
and combat restoration remains incomplete. All 13 differing bytes now have
identified native writer paths (12 self-pointer bytes plus this flag byte).
## Auxiliary flag consumer identified

Vtable slot +38 (RVA 31F4E0, a leaf routine not listed in unwind metadata)
returns object+3C0. Update helper 32EB50 obtains that subobject and first checks
its +8 pointer (object+3C8); if null, it returns at 32EB64 BEFORE reading the flag.
With a non-null pointer, 32EB85 checks subobject+10 (object+3D0). Nonzero selects
an initialization helper according to object+A4 (32EC40, 32EDA0, or 32EE30).
Only successful initialization clears the flag at 32EBCD, then normal update
processing continues. This supports describing it as a pending-initialization
flag, not padding. Example mode-3 initialization resets progress/cached fields;
all modes' behavior has not been proven equivalent to preserved state.

In the traced A+G snapshot, object+A4=0, object+3C0=0, and object+3C8=0 before
AND after loading. Thus the observed flag difference does not cause work in
32EB50 while that pointer remains null. This is a conditional static finding,
not proof it cannot affect a later transition. Clearing it universally would
skip initialization in active cases and is not justified.

Evidence: work/aux-flag-consumer-confirmed.txt includes getter, consumer/clear,
and an initializer; broader searches in work/aux-flag-consumers.txt and
work/aux-related-consumers.txt. No live test, installation, or correction-range
change made in this investigation. Next narrow validation should track all
auxiliary initialization flags and related pointer identities across restore
and subsequent frames, retaining native initialization until active versus
inactive cases are understood. Do not claim full combat state equivalence.

## Subsystem identification and corrected progress interpretation

Pinned executable MSVC RTTI identifies vtable RVA3E88858 as
.?AVH_CameraFuncThrow@@ (H_CameraFuncThrow). Complete Object Locator RVA3E9F108
has type-descriptor RVA68433312 (decimal); its name is stored at descriptor+16.
Constructor322610 places this camera object at auxiliary buffer+F80 / slot4.
This is a camera subsystem, not fighter animation/simulation progress.

Activation327F20 is suppressed by global4846360 or owner+9C==0x13; otherwise
sets global40F4A84, selects a path from object+3B0 bits (some cases randomized),
sets +388 to0/1 and enables +30. Update328BC0 checks owner-selected object's+B4,
global40F4A84 and +30, then dispatches +388==1 to32ADC0 (other path32B380).
Captured Soul Charge has activation bits0x78001 and +388==1, matching this path.
These numeric conditions are not assigned unsupported game-menu meanings.

Crucial correction: mode3 evaluation32E3F0 reads FLOAT object+28 at32E451 to
choose adjacent sequence records and interpolate. The previously monitored
+2DC and target+1E0 fields are not this mode's sequence position. Their zero
values do not show that the camera sequence failed to progress. The earlier
nonzero-progress search was targeting the wrong fields for this mode.

Existing serialized captures already contain nonzero camera sequence position:

## Camera output reconstruction investigation
Static evaluator32E3F0 obtains target from camera object+38 and copies object fields+50/+54/+58/+64/+70/+78 to target+230/+234/+238/+244/+250/+258 at32E9F7..32EA33. Serialized before/after blobs in charge-preserve-21016-27791531 preserve all six source values exactly. Each next-frame output matches its saved source exactly. The three baseline differences (+230/+238/+244) already exist before load and are restored from the preserved per-activation source values; they are not evidence of a restore-induced mismatch.

The measured target+1F0 address is not covered by a before-save source transfer. Output fields are reset during the probe and reconstructed during the following update: 14 of17 measured outputs match the baseline at aligned position/state/path/enabled, while the remaining three match their own preserved sources. Camera position advances1->2 normally. This supports treating these measured mode3 outputs as derived data in this tested case, rather than adding speculative preservation writes. It does not establish ordering safety for every consumer between load and camera evaluation, other camera modes, or delayed rollback.

Initializer31BC50 writes target+220 default0x425B1893, matching the observed after-probe value1113266323; its exact invocation chain during load has not been proven. ## Changing buffer addresses traced
Writer30A980 saves through player+35A0 and player+27760 virtual+28 accessors (argument0), lengths1840/800, returns30A9F0/30AA12. Reader30AE80 uses the same accessors for destinations (returns30AF28/30AF4B), after reading player+10 length80 and player+90 length3510. Constructors303841/303857 install vtables3E87638/3E87700. Virtual+28 resolves30B7B0/30B680: return subobject+28 pointer plus index*64. These are preallocated triple buffers, not arbitrary allocation movement: initialization30B7D0/30B6E0 sets three128-byte-aligned slots at stridesC000/800. Rotation30B630 updates index=(index+2)%3, previous(+30)=current(+28), current=slots[index]. Audit source addresses exactly match slot2->1 for35A0 and slot1->0 for27760, both players.

No before-save transfer overlaps either subobject's38-byte header in this audit; current/previous slot selectors are not directly serialized. Native reader therefore selects through the live current-slot pointer rather than a recorded destination. This explains source-address mismatch and rules out treating equality as a necessary generic guard. It does NOT prove delayed-load correctness: previous-slot consumers and unrecorded portions (large slotC000 vs saved1840 prefix) remain relevant. Preserve this distinction; do not simply rewind slot pointers or copy entire buffers without validating consumers.

Evidence work/delay-buffer-writer.txt, delay-buffer-reader.txt, delay-buffer-references.txt, delay-buffer-selectors.txt, delay-buffer-accessors.txt, delay-buffer-rotation.txt; captures/delay-audit-54024-30659890-buffer-slot-analysis.json and -buffer-header-coverage.json. Static analysis and saved artifacts only; no live modifications. Next implementation should measure/guard slot headers and previous-slot use before enabling a one-frame idle delayed load, and compare the replayed next frame against its original saved counterpart. Delayed load remains disabled.

## Previous-slot consumers and delayed-probe requirements
Static3043D0 obtains large-ring element1 from previous(+38 accessor) and current(+28 accessor). At304438..3044D4 it subtracts previous element offsets30/34/38 from current and writes results to player180/184/188, then filters into170/174/178 and conditionally1C0/1C8. Thus previous-slot data can feed player state, not merely rendering; exact runtime reachability/order at our idle boundary remains unproven. Another site2F9252..2F9261 stores a previous-slot base pointer into an object's+20A8, demonstrating potential cached aliases.

305B50 calls virtual40 on both rings at305B82/305B8F; both vtables resolve40 to30B630 (rotation). Player-update path2DCC25 calls305B50; there are additional setup paths2D49CE/2D49DA and2DB9E4/2DB9F0. This does not prove every consumer occurs after rotation on every path. Evidence work/delay-previous-consumers.txt, delay-consumer-order.txt, delay-boundary-order.txt.

Delayed load must not assume current-slot native serialization is complete. Proposed supplemental capture: both players, both ring headers/index/current/previous, all three slot contents (3*C000+3*800 per player,307200 total bytes), and validated vtables/slot-address geometry. First measure these read-only across adjacent frames to establish consistent rotation, stable ownership and previous/current relationships. A future restore must handle cached aliases/other consumers and preserve physical-slot contents consistently; no header rewinding or blanket pointer substitution justified yet. Native large-ring save covers1840 ofC000, so prefix-only equality is insufficient. Replay validation also needs the original next-frame native/supplemental snapshot and identical update inputs: entry2DBC60 reads pointed-to input/timing data, so reusing only its argument address is insufficient. No DLL edits, installation, or live writes in this investigation; older-state load remains disabled.

## One-frame idle rewind experiment implemented
Adds DelayRestore with FullRestore transport, exclusive of other modes; OFFLINE_DELAY_RESTORE. Builds two adjacent idle saves A/B using audited ring captures. Records40 bytes of pointed-to input/timing data (8+8+24 from entry argument pointers0/8/16) and requires equality across three boundaries; this is measured argument coverage, not proof of all external inputs. Requires full preflight, idle, exact outer+1, stable player roots/subobject source pointers, unchanged RNG between A/B, unchanged native save effects on measured fields/rings, unchanged serialization shape/writer sites; changed sources must be exactly captured current ring prefixes. Rejects before loading on these guard failures.

Restores all physical ring slots and selectors to A before native load so native pointer fixups see A's slot selection. Ring restore validates all four owners/geometry before any writes. Native load must consume all transfers and match A's selected keys/player roots; then existing idle scalar/RNG corrections run, and full ring history is reapplied/verified. Next normal game update executes; third boundary saves replay state and compares against original B's native bytes, keys and full ring contents. Reports raw replay differences without suppressing rebuilt-pointer changes. Restore is once/process; failures stop capture and require restart. Partial native/write failures are not transactionally undone. An expected repeated outer value after successful rewind will appear in recorder discontinuity count; inspect explicit delayed/replay results.

Scope one_frame_idle_restore_experiment distinguishes report semantics: before.bin=A, after.bin=original B (not post-load); replay.bin=next normal update after restoring A; replay-rings.bin=its physical slots. Original A/B four ring checkpoints retained. Measurement2 becomes loaded A and4 preserved A; measurement3 remains original B after-save. Legacy post_save/raw_byte_differences fields must not be treated as replay equality; use replay_* fields. Cached aliases outside native reconstruction, unmeasured state, thread consumers and input coverage remain experimental. No claim of general deterministic replay or network rollback.

Local tests cover full mocked adjacent-save/restore/next-frame comparison, unchanged input requirement/no-load rejection, once-only completion, and existing native/buffer rejection checks. Four suites and native DLL build passed. Live installation/capture pending.

## Network input observation implementation (2026-10-08)

Added a separately armed, once-per-process 120-record input observer at the pinned
pre-update boundary. It copies the 40-byte input envelope, outer counter and QPC
into bounded shared memory. The callback returns before all save/load and input
replacement paths. The companion opens the mapping read-only, exchanges records
through the authenticated relay, and exports local/remote CSVs. PASS is transport
integrity only; game alignment and player mapping are explicitly unverified.

a relay, including every-fifth-packet loss. No new real-game observation has run.
